# Historical author response routes for R125

All 35 genuine reports remain authoritative in their original source and experiment scope. This companion maps **620 explicit major blocks and 64 overlapping parent sections**; those are 684 index records, not independent issues or completed revisions. The inherited 1,014 finer item responses and overlapping parent treatments are retained separately.

The JSON companion preserves the exact individual prior author action and remaining requirement for every major block, original request-body hashes, report identities and Git-bound retained source routes. Current R125 links identify relevant amendments; they do not declare the original requirement closed. The current 30-request response distinguishes the new exact support theorem, the separate operational experiment and the outstanding scientific demands.

### M6. Design policy-cost inference around an economic margin

**Numbered child 1.** The statement that the centered penalty has infimum zero without attainment is correct; the implementation section should repeatedly distinguish this from the finite case in which a maximizing piece exists.

Retained author action: The inherited centered-risk proof retains nonnegative penalty with infimum zero without assuming attainment. The new coupled-risk result distinguishes an arbitrary feasible reference density with a paid slack from an optimizing density; its compactness or measurable-selection premises are explicit when attainment is used. Requirement recorded at that source: No finite risk-piece maximum is substituted for an unattained general dual supremum.

**Numbered child 2.** The terminology “risk occupation” should always be accompanied by “risk-distorted” when there is any possibility of confusion with physical or empirical occupation.

Retained author action: The retained centered-risk section calls these risk-distorted occupations and explicitly distinguishes them from frequencies under the physical law. New signed recursive transport uses the original conditional risk kernel and does not interpret its weights as empirical state counts. Requirement recorded at that source: Physical-law inference and risk-distorted transport continue to require distinct assumptions and records.

**Numbered child 3.** The theorem's measurable-selection and bounded-domain assumptions should be gathered in one formal assumption block rather than distributed between prose and inherited sections.

Retained author action: The foundations collect compact feasibility, continuity, boundedness, measurable feedback and operator assumptions in formal blocks. The coupled supplement states the original risk envelope, admissible densities and selection premises beside the theorem. Inherited centered-risk proofs remain attached to their own bounded-domain assumptions. Requirement recorded at that source: A general fully coupled FBSDE or unbounded-domain theorem is not obtained by omitting these assumptions.

**Numbered child 4.** The paper should report the distribution, not only the maximum, of scalar versus state-dependent tightening across all 252 interval configurations. Include no-improvement, no-cut, and loose-bound cases in the main diagnostic summary.

Retained author action: The complete 252-configuration R92 diagnostic and its zero-radius, no-cut and unchanged cases are retained as deterministic evidence. New A/B/C tables likewise retain every cell, including no policy change and slower service. The current tail theorem does not retroactively transform the R92 maximum table into a distributional study. Requirement recorded at that source: A newly generated full 252-row tightening distribution is not claimed here; the requested historical distribution summary remains open.

**Numbered child 5.** Report the total number of candidate risk pieces before and after cuts, the time to prove cuts, and the complete time saved downstream. A cut count without a denominator is hard to interpret.

Retained author action: The original cut count is kept as a count of excluded feasible risk pieces, not a speedup. New masking and fused-tail studies report raw query counts, cutoff evaluation work, fallback and full service clocks. The B ablation demonstrates directly why fewer evaluated pieces need not reduce elapsed service time. Requirement recorded at that source: All historical R92 pre/post candidate denominators and downstream clock savings require their own reconstruction; R119 counts cannot fill them.

**Numbered child 6.** The bit-length table is useful. Add numerator/denominator growth by operation and identify the point at which exact arithmetic becomes less efficient than outward floating or interval arithmetic.

Retained author action: The exact R92 bit-length maximum remains a diagnostic of the executed rational records. The new theorem explicitly states an exact point-information bound and excludes bit-complexity claims, while the production service uses outward arithmetic and has separately audited Fraction fixtures. Requirement recorded at that source: Operation-by-operation numerator/denominator growth and a measured rational-versus-outward crossover remain unexecuted.

**Numbered child 7.** The absorbing example should be labeled explicitly as zero policy loss with a nonzero continuation bias, so readers do not confuse certificate sharpness with value-function accuracy.

**Numbered child 1.** State `N^{-2}` consistently as an error-versus-information rate; “information exponent two” is easy to misread.

Retained author action: The current theorem and response use error-versus-information rate for the N^\{-2\} width order. They distinguish risk width, constrained action gap, actual regret and observed work counts and state the separate original-investment action-transfer result. Requirement recorded at that source: No unresolved terminology exception is used to infer a policy-regret rate directly from the width theorem.

**Numbered child 2.** Put “deterministic, point-value, univariate, fixed-alpha” adjacent to every high-level summary of the lower bound.

Retained author action: High-level summaries state deterministic exact point values, univariate uniform risk support and a known convex Lipschitz class. The new result improves the historical fixed-alpha claim to alpha-uniform constants for N at least 32, while retaining the limits concerning bit cost, formula access and multiple innovations. Requirement recorded at that source: The theorem does not cover every stronger information oracle or randomized algorithm.

**Numbered child 3.** Discuss the opposite tail-level behavior of the lower and upper constants as `alpha` approaches one.

Retained author action: The mismatch is addressed mathematically: paid tail localization and selected-tail quadrature give an upper width proportional to KR(1-alpha)N^\{-2\}, matching the retained lower order in both information and tail mass. The proof handles alpha=0 separately, excludes alpha=1 in normalized tails, and charges sign queries. Requirement recorded at that source: Exact point-information order is not a bit-complexity theorem as alpha approaches one.

The fixed current models preserve their restrictions and exact source identities.
